# G1: maker history and joint reading

What must a reader know about a maker's habits, and when does another probabilistic pass help? In the 60-cell map, four passes using only one page hurt all six small-world settings, losing 0.359 to 1.799 nats of true-goal log score versus one pass. With 16 earlier documents, the own-history one-pass reader comes within 0.05 nats of the joint reader in 4 of six small-world settings and 4 of six larger-world settings. These are descriptive constructed-method results, not evidence about people or automatic engine admission.

History lengths share the same page, maker, library and nested documents. Every standard error uses whole library blocks; pooled token AUC uses midranks and delete-one-block jackknife uncertainty. Exact joint inference has the expected proper-score ceiling, full-library beams equal it, and the typical-habit reader is invariant to history. Those identities do not fix the measured absolute performance levels.

First table: one-page loop cost, the first sampled history meeting each descriptive comparison, typical-habit loss after 16 earlier documents, and paired token-mark discrimination. Threshold crossings below use point estimates, not a claim test.

Admission retains the original fixed-tolerance failure. The owner approved max(original tolerance, three combined library-block standard errors), using the supplied scripts and seeds to reconstruct baseline uncertainty. All 176 score means reproduce at printed precision and all 292 applicable comparisons pass. Sixteen old-setting AUC ranges are inapplicable; eight comparable low-overlap points use the supplied original output. That admission left the exact known-answer gates and scientific source unchanged; the subsequent numerical repair is described below.

Numerical repair: the original whole-grid reconstruction failed on empirical goal ties and token ranks, and is preserved. Exact integer-count symmetries now receive equal goal probabilities, with the lowest goal index breaking the tie. Mark scores use twelve decimal places before exact midranking; the largest change in AUC when using ten places is 1.22e-06. The same seeds and grid were rerun in a separate capsule, with unchanged verification tolerances and a repeated 292-comparison pre-flight check. This repairs numerical representation; it does not add worlds or change reader laws. The architectures remain untested miniatures.
